88. Merge Sorted Array Easy

LeetCode · cf/Merge_Sorted_Array.cpp

// orz
// 88. Merge Sorted Array · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// nums1 and nums2 are sorted in non-decreasing order. nums1 has length m + n:
// its first m elements are real, the last n are 0 and only hold space.
// nums2 has n elements. merge nums2 into nums1 so that nums1 is sorted.
// return nothing, the answer is nums1 itself.
// Example 1: nums1 = [1,2,3,0,0,0], m = 3, nums2 = [2,5,6], n = 3 -> [1,2,2,3,5,6]
// Example 2: nums1 = [1], m = 1, nums2 = [], n = 0             -> [1]
// Example 3: nums1 = [0], m = 0, nums2 = [1], n = 1            -> [1]
// Constraints: nums1.length = m + n, nums2.length = n, 0 <= m, n <= 200,
//              1 <= m + n <= 200, -10^9 <= nums1[i], nums2[j] <= 10^9
// Follow up: an algorithm that runs in O(m + n) time. -> done here.
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    a = nums1[0 .. m - 1], b = nums2[0 .. n - 1], both sorted.
//    write the sorted order of the multiset a + b into nums1[0 .. m + n - 1],
//    with no second array.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    copy nums2 into nums1[m .. m + n - 1] and sort nums1.
//    O((m + n) log(m + n)). fast enough here, but it throws away that both parts are already sorted.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the sort compares elements whose order is already known inside a and inside b.
//    the only unknown is how a and b interleave.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    the merge of two sorted lists: compare the two heads, take the right one.
//    from the front it breaks, because writing into nums1[0 ..] overwrites a elements
//    that are not read yet. from the BACK it is safe: the free slots sit at the end
//    of nums1, so fill nums1 from the last slot with the larger of the two tails.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : i = m - 1 (last unread element of a), j = n - 1 (last unread element of b),
//             k = m + n - 1 (the slot to fill next).
//    step 2 : while b still has elements (j >= 0):
//             if a still has elements and nums1[i] > nums2[j], put nums1[i] in slot k, i--.
//             else put nums2[j] in slot k, j--.
//             then k--.
//    step 3 : when b is empty, nums1[0 .. i] are the smallest elements of a and already
//             sit in their place, so stop.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    slot k gets the largest element not placed yet, so slots fill from the back in sorted order.
//    no unread element of a is ever overwritten: k = i + j + 1 and j >= 0, so k > i.
//    time O(m + n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    void merge(vector<int>& nums1, int m, vector<int>& nums2, int n) {
        // step 1 : three pointers from the back
        ll i = m - 1 ;     // last unread element of nums1
        ll j = n - 1 ;     // last unread element of nums2
        ll k = m + n - 1 ; // the slot to fill next
        // step 2 : the larger tail goes into slot k
        while(j >= 0)
        {
            if(i >= 0 && nums1[i] > nums2[j])
            {
                nums1[k] = nums1[i] ;
                i-- ;
            }
            else
            {
                nums1[k] = nums2[j] ;
                j-- ;
            }
            k-- ;
        }
        // step 3 : nums1[0 .. i] is already in place
    }
};
27. Remove Element Easy

LeetCode · cf/Remove_Element.cpp

// orz
// 27. Remove Element · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// given an integer array nums and an integer val, remove every occurrence of val
// in place. the order of the remaining elements may change. return k, the number
// of elements that are not val; the first k elements of nums must be exactly
// those elements (in any order), what is after them does not matter.
// Example 1: nums = [3,2,2,3], val = 3           -> 2, nums = [2,2,_,_]
// Example 2: nums = [0,1,2,2,3,0,4,2], val = 2   -> 5, nums = [0,1,4,0,3,_,_,_] (any order)
// Constraints: 0 <= n <= 100, 0 <= nums[i] <= 50, 0 <= val <= 100
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    k = number of i with nums[i] != val.
//    rearrange nums so that nums[0 .. k - 1] is the multiset { nums[i] : nums[i] != val }.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    find a val, shift everything after it one place left, repeat.
//    each shift is O(n), up to n shifts, O(n^2).
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    every element after a val is moved again for every val before it,
//    when it only needs to move once, straight to its final slot.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    treat nums[0 .. k - 1] as a stack of the kept elements, built in place.
//    read nums left to right; an element that is not val is pushed, a val is skipped.
//    the write slot k never passes the read slot i, so nothing unread is overwritten.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : k = 0, the stack is empty.
//    step 2 : for i = 0 .. n - 1:
//             if nums[i] == val, skip it.
//             else push it: nums[k] = nums[i], then k++.
//    step 3 : return k.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    every non-val element is pushed exactly once, in order, and no val is pushed,
//    so nums[0 .. k - 1] is exactly the kept elements. k <= i at each write,
//    so nums[i] is read before slot i can be written.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int removeElement(vector<int>& nums, int val) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1 : nums[0 .. k - 1] is the stack of kept elements
        ll k = 0 ;
        // step 2 : skip val, push everything else
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            if(nums[i] == val)
            {
                continue ;
            }
            else
            {
                nums[k] = nums[i] ;
                k++ ;
            }
        }
        // step 3
        return k ;
    }
};
26. Remove Duplicates from Sorted Array Easy

LeetCode · cf/Remove_Duplicates_from_Sorted_Array.cpp

// orz
// 26. Remove Duplicates from Sorted Array · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// nums is sorted in non-decreasing order. remove the duplicates in place so that
// each unique element appears only once, keeping their relative order.
// return k, the number of unique elements; the first k elements of nums must be
// the unique elements in order, what is after them does not matter.
// Example 1: nums = [1,1,2]                -> 2, nums = [1,2,_]
// Example 2: nums = [0,0,1,1,1,2,2,3,3,4]  -> 5, nums = [0,1,2,3,4,_,_,_,_,_]
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 3 * 10^4, -100 <= nums[i] <= 100, nums is sorted non-decreasing
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    k = number of distinct values in nums.
//    make nums[0 .. k - 1] the distinct values in increasing order.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    find a value equal to the one before it, shift everything after it one place left, repeat.
//    up to n shifts of O(n) each, O(n^2) = 9 * 10^8. too slow.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    every element is shifted again for every duplicate before it,
//    when it only needs to move once, straight to its final slot.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    treat nums[0 .. k - 1] as a stack of the answer, built in place (my stack template).
//    the array is sorted, so all copies of a value are next to each other:
//    x is a new value exactly when the stack is empty or its top nums[k - 1] != x.
//    so only the top has to be checked, never the whole stack.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : k = 0, the stack is empty.
//    step 2 : for i = 0 .. n - 1, x = nums[i]:
//             if the stack is empty or nums[k - 1] != x, push x: nums[k] = x, then k++.
//             else x is a copy of the top, skip it.
//    step 3 : return k.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    sorted means equal values form one block, so the first element of every block
//    is pushed and every later copy sees itself on top and is skipped.
//    k <= i at each write, so nums[i] is read before slot i can be written.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int removeDuplicates(vector<int>& nums) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1 : nums[0 .. k - 1] is the stack, nums[k - 1] is the top
        ll k = 0 ;
        // step 2 : push x only when it differs from the top
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            ll x = nums[i] ;
            if(k == 0 || nums[k - 1] != x)
            {
                nums[k] = x ;
                k++ ;
            }
            else
            {
                continue ;
            }
        }
        // step 3
        return k ;
    }
};
80. Remove Duplicates from Sorted Array II Medium

LeetCode · cf/Remove_Duplicates_from_Sorted_Array_II.cpp

// orz
// 80. Remove Duplicates from Sorted Array II · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// nums is sorted in non-decreasing order. remove some duplicates in place so that
// each unique element appears at most twice, keeping the relative order.
// return k, the length of the result; the first k elements of nums must be the
// result, what is after them does not matter. use O(1) extra memory.
// Example 1: nums = [1,1,1,2,2,3]         -> 5, nums = [1,1,2,2,3,_]
// Example 2: nums = [0,0,1,1,1,1,2,3,3]   -> 7, nums = [0,0,1,1,2,3,3,_,_]
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 3 * 10^4, -10^4 <= nums[i] <= 10^4, nums is sorted non-decreasing
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    for every value x with c copies, keep min(c , 2) copies.
//    nums[0 .. k - 1] = the kept elements in order, k = sum of min(c , 2) over all values.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    find a third copy (nums[i] == nums[i - 2]), shift everything after it one place left, repeat.
//    up to n shifts of O(n) each, O(n^2) = 9 * 10^8. too slow.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    every element is shifted again for every extra copy before it,
//    when it only needs to move once, straight to its final slot.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    treat nums[0 .. k - 1] as a stack of the answer, built in place: my stack template
//    (pop while the top breaks the rule, then push).
//    the rule: never three equal values in a row. the array is sorted, so all copies of x
//    are next to each other, and x breaks the rule exactly when the top two are x.
//    so only the top two have to be checked.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : k = 0, the stack is empty. nums[k - 1] is the top, nums[k - 2] is below it.
//    step 2 : for i = 0 .. n - 1, x = nums[i]:
//             while the stack has 2 elements and the top two are both x, pop: k--.
//             (it pops at most once: after one pop the top two are not both x any more.)
//    step 3 : push x: nums[k] = x, then k++.
//    step 4 : return k.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    when the top two are x, step 2 pops one x and step 3 pushes x back, so the stack
//    is unchanged: the third, fourth, .. copy of x is dropped. otherwise x is pushed.
//    so each value keeps its first min(c , 2) copies, in order.
//    k <= i at each write, so nums[i] is read before slot i can be written.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int removeDuplicates(vector<int>& nums) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1 : nums[0 .. k - 1] is the stack
        ll k = 0 ;
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            ll x = nums[i] ;
            // step 2 : pop while the top two are already x
            while(k >= 2 && nums[k - 1] == x && nums[k - 2] == x)
            {
                k-- ;
            }
            // step 3 : push x
            nums[k] = x ;
            k++ ;
        }
        // step 4
        return k ;
    }
};
169. Majority Element Easy

LeetCode · cf/Majority_Element.cpp

// orz
// 169. Majority Element · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// given an array nums of size n, return the majority element: the element that
// appears more than n / 2 times (rounded down). the majority element always exists.
// Example 1: nums = [3,2,3]           -> 3
// Example 2: nums = [2,2,1,1,1,2,2]   -> 2
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 5 * 10^4, -10^9 <= nums[i] <= 10^9
// Follow up: O(n) time and O(1) space. -> done here (Boyer-Moore voting).
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    find the value m with count(m) > n / 2. it is unique: two such values
//    would need more than n elements together.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    for every i, count how many j have nums[j] == nums[i]. stop when the count > n / 2.
//    O(n^2) = 2.5 * 10^9. too slow.
//    (a map of counts is O(n log n) and O(n) space, it misses the follow up.)
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    it counts every value exactly, but I only need to know which value wins
//    a "more than half" vote, not by how much.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    cancel pairs of DIFFERENT values. throw away two different elements: m still has
//    more than half of what is left, because at most one of the two was m.
//    keep cancelling, and whatever survives must be m.
//    the cancelling is done in one pass with a candidate cand and a counter cnt
//    = how many uncancelled copies of cand are waiting.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : cand = anything, cnt = 0 (nothing is waiting).
//    step 2 : for every x in nums:
//             if cnt == 0, nothing is waiting, so x becomes the new cand.
//    step 3 : if x == cand, it waits with the others: cnt++.
//             else x cancels one waiting copy of cand: cnt--.
//    step 4 : return cand.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    at the end, cnt copies of cand are still waiting, and every other element is in a
//    cancelled pair of two different values. each pair holds at most one m.
//    if cand != m, every copy of m is in a pair, so count(m) <= number of pairs <= n / 2,
//    a contradiction. so cand = m.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int majorityElement(vector<int>& nums) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1
        ll cand = 0 ; // this stores the value whose copies are waiting
        ll cnt = 0 ;  // this stores how many copies of cand are not cancelled yet
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            ll x = nums[i] ;
            // step 2 : nothing waiting, x is the new candidate
            if(cnt == 0)
            {
                cand = x ;
            }
            // step 3 : same value waits, different value cancels one copy
            if(x == cand)
            {
                cnt++ ;
            }
            else
            {
                cnt-- ;
            }
        }
        // step 4
        return cand ;
    }
};
189. Rotate Array Medium

LeetCode · cf/Rotate_Array.cpp

// orz
// 189. Rotate Array · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// given an integer array nums, rotate it to the right by k steps (k >= 0):
// every element moves k places right, and elements falling off the end come back at the front.
// Example 1: nums = [1,2,3,4,5,6,7], k = 3   -> [5,6,7,1,2,3,4]
// Example 2: nums = [-1,-100,3,99], k = 2    -> [3,99,-1,-100]
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 10^5, -2^31 <= nums[i] <= 2^31 - 1, 0 <= k <= 10^5
// Follow up: do it in place with O(1) extra space. -> done here.
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    new[(i + k) mod n] = old[i] for every i, with O(1) extra space.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    rotate by 1 step, k times: save the last element, shift everything right by one,
//    put it at the front. O(n * k) = 10^10. dead.
//    (a copy array is O(n) time but O(n) space, it misses the follow up.)
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    every element walks k single steps, when it only has to land once in its final slot.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    rotating by n is doing nothing, so k = k mod n first.
//    cut nums into A = the first n - k elements and B = the last k. rotating right by k
//    turns A B into B A. reversing the whole array gives rev(B) rev(A): the blocks are
//    already in the right place, only their insides are backwards. so reverse each block again.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : k = k mod n.
//    step 2 : reverse all of nums. now it is rev(B) rev(A), rev(B) = nums[0 .. k - 1].
//    step 3 : reverse nums[0 .. k - 1], it becomes B.
//    step 4 : reverse nums[k .. n - 1], it becomes A. now nums = B A.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    reversing a whole array maps index i to n - 1 - i, so the last block B lands at the
//    front and A at the back, each in backwards order. reversing a block twice gives it back,
//    so steps 3 and 4 fix the insides. 3 reversals, each element is swapped at most twice.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    void rotate(vector<int>& nums, int k) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1 : rotating by n does nothing
        ll r = k % n ;
        // step 2 : A B -> rev(B) rev(A)
        reverse(nums.begin() , nums.end()) ;
        // step 3 : rev(B) -> B
        reverse(nums.begin() , nums.begin() + r) ;
        // step 4 : rev(A) -> A
        reverse(nums.begin() + r , nums.end()) ;
    }
};
121. Best Time to Buy and Sell Stock Easy

LeetCode · cf/Best_Time_to_Buy_and_Sell_Stock.cpp

// orz
// 121. Best Time to Buy and Sell Stock · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// prices[i] is the price of a stock on day i. choose one day to buy one share and a
// later day to sell it. return the maximum profit, or 0 if no profit is possible.
// Example 1: prices = [7,1,5,3,6,4]   -> 5   (buy at 1 on day 1, sell at 6 on day 4)
// Example 2: prices = [7,6,4,3,1]     -> 0   (prices only fall, don't trade)
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 10^5, 0 <= prices[i] <= 10^4
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    maximise prices[j] - prices[i] over all i < j, and 0 if that max is negative
//    (or there is no pair, n = 1).
//
// 2. Brute force
//    try every pair i < j. O(n^2) = 10^10. dead.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    for a fixed sell day j, only the cheapest buy day before j matters,
//    and the brute force scans all of them again for every j.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    max difference with i < j: fix the sell day j, the best buy is
//    minsofar = min(prices[0 .. j - 1]). that min grows one element per day,
//    so keep it as a running min.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : ans = 0 (not trading is allowed), minsofar = prices[0].
//    step 2 : for j = 1 .. n - 1:
//             score selling on day j FIRST: ans = max(ans , prices[j] - minsofar).
//             then minsofar = min(minsofar , prices[j]).
//             first score, then update, because buy and sell can't be the same day
//             (that pair gives 0 anyway, ans already starts at 0).
//    step 3 : return ans.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    when day j is scored, minsofar = min(prices[0 .. j - 1]), the best buy for selling on j.
//    every sell day is scored, so the best pair is found.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int maxProfit(vector<int>& prices) {
        ll n = prices.size() ;
        // step 1
        ll ans = 0 ;
        ll minsofar = prices[0] ; // this stores the cheapest price before day j
        // step 2 : score day j, then update the min
        for(ll j = 1 ; j < n ; j++)
        {
            ans = max(ans , prices[j] - minsofar) ;
            minsofar = min(minsofar , (ll)prices[j]) ;
        }
        // step 3
        return ans ;
    }
};
122. Best Time to Buy and Sell Stock II Medium

LeetCode · cf/Best_Time_to_Buy_and_Sell_Stock_II.cpp

// orz
// 122. Best Time to Buy and Sell Stock II · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// prices[i] is the price of a stock on day i. on each day you may buy and/or sell,
// but you can hold at most one share at any time. you may buy and sell on the same day.
// return the maximum profit.
// Example 1: prices = [7,1,5,3,6,4]   -> 7   (buy 1 sell 5: +4, buy 3 sell 6: +3)
// Example 2: prices = [1,2,3,4,5]     -> 4   (buy 1 sell 5)
// Example 3: prices = [7,6,4,3,1]     -> 0
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 3 * 10^4, 0 <= prices[i] <= 10^4
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    choose trades (b1 , s1) , (b2 , s2) , .. with b1 < s1 <= b2 < s2 <= .. (one share at a time).
//    maximise the sum of prices[s] - prices[b].
//
// 2. Brute force
//    on every day choose hold or not hold, try all 2^n patterns. n = 3 * 10^4. dead.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the profit of a trade does not depend on how it is cut into days.
//    every pattern pays the same day-to-day moves again and again.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    a trade split into days: prices[s] - prices[b] = sum of d[t] = prices[t + 1] - prices[t]
//    for t = b .. s - 1. trades don't overlap, so any plan earns sum of d[t] over some
//    set of days t, each t at most once. that is at most the sum of the positive d[t].
//    and that bound is reached: on every day with d[t] > 0, buy on day t and sell on day t + 1.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : ans = 0.
//    step 2 : for t = 0 .. n - 2: d = prices[t + 1] - prices[t].
//             if d > 0, take it: ans += d. else skip that day.
//    step 3 : return ans.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    upper bound: every plan's profit = sum of d[t] over the days it holds, <= sum of positive d[t].
//    reached: the one-day trades on up days are a valid plan (selling and buying again
//    on the same day is allowed) and earn exactly that sum.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int maxProfit(vector<int>& prices) {
        ll n = prices.size() ;
        // step 1
        ll ans = 0 ;
        // step 2 : take every positive day-to-day move
        for(ll t = 0 ; t + 1 < n ; t++)
        {
            ll d = prices[t + 1] - prices[t] ;
            if(d > 0)
            {
                ans += d ;
            }
            else
            {
                continue ;
            }
        }
        // step 3
        return ans ;
    }
};
55. Jump Game Medium

LeetCode · cf/Jump_Game.cpp

// orz
// 55. Jump Game · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// you start at index 0 of an integer array nums. nums[i] is the maximum jump length
// from index i: from i you can jump to any of i + 1 .. i + nums[i].
// return true if you can reach the last index, else false.
// Example 1: nums = [2,3,1,1,4]   -> true   (0 -> 1 -> 4)
// Example 2: nums = [3,2,1,0,4]   -> false  (every path gets stuck at index 3, nums[3] = 0)
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 10^4, 0 <= nums[i] <= 10^5
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    graph on 0 .. n - 1 with edges i -> j for i < j <= i + nums[i].
//    is n - 1 reachable from 0?
//
// 2. Brute force
//    bfs / dfs on that graph. node i has nums[i] edges, so O(sum of nums[i]) = 10^4 * 10^5 = 10^9.
//    too slow.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    it treats every reachable index as a separate fact, but the reachable set has a shape:
//    it is always a prefix 0 .. far.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    if j is reachable, every index before j is reachable too: the jump that landed on j
//    came from some i < j with j <= i + nums[i], and that jump could stop on any index
//    between i and j instead (and indices <= i are handled the same way, by induction).
//    (same model as Jump Game II: a long jump plus free steps back i -> i - 1.)
//    so the reachable set is 0 .. far, and I only keep the number far.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : far = 0, only index 0 is reached.
//    step 2 : for i = 0 .. n - 1:
//             if i > far, index i is not reachable, and nothing after it is either. return false.
//    step 3 : else i is reachable, so all of i + 1 .. i + nums[i] is too:
//             far = max(far , i + nums[i]).
//    step 4 : the loop got past n - 1, so n - 1 <= far. return true.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    after processing 0 .. i (all reachable), far = max of j + nums[j] over j <= i,
//    the furthest index any of them can jump to, and by the prefix fact of 4 every index
//    up to far is reachable. if i > far, no index before i jumps to i or past it,
//    so i and everything after it is unreachable.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    bool canJump(vector<int>& nums) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1 : this stores the last index of the reachable prefix 0 .. far
        ll far = 0 ;
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            // step 2 : i is past the reachable prefix
            if(i > far)
            {
                return false ;
            }
            // step 3 : i is reachable, extend the prefix
            else
            {
                far = max(far , i + nums[i]) ;
            }
        }
        // step 4
        return true ;
    }
};
45. Jump Game II Medium

LeetCode · cf/Jump_Game_II.cpp

// orz
// 45. Jump Game II · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// you start at index 0 of an integer array nums. nums[i] is the maximum jump length
// from index i: from i you can jump to any of i + 1 .. i + nums[i] (staying inside the array).
// return the minimum number of jumps to reach index n - 1. it is always reachable.
// Example 1: nums = [2,3,1,1,4]   -> 2   (0 -> 1 -> 4)
// Example 2: nums = [2,3,0,1,4]   -> 2
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 10^4, 0 <= nums[i] <= 1000, n - 1 is reachable from 0
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    graph on 0 .. n - 1 with an edge i -> j of cost 1 for every i < j <= i + nums[i].
//    find the shortest distance from 0 to n - 1.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    plain bfs on that graph. node i has nums[i] edges, so O(sum of nums[i]) = 10^4 * 1000 = 10^7 edges,
//    built and stored. (trying every jump sequence by recursion is exponential.)
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the nums[i] edges out of i all cost 1 and land on a whole block i + 1 .. i + nums[i].
//    landing short of the far end is the same as landing on the far end and walking back.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    replace the nums[i] edges of i with just two:
//      i -> min(n - 1 , i + nums[i])   cost 1   (the longest jump)
//      i -> i - 1                      cost 0   (walk back for free)
//    "jump to i + d" = "jump to the far end, then d' free steps back", so every old path
//    still exists with the same cost. and the free steps never make anything cheaper:
//    the set reached with <= c jumps is always a prefix 0 .. R (Jump Game), so walking back
//    only visits indices that already cost <= c.
//    now it is a shortest path with weights 0 and 1: 0-1 bfs with a deque.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : build g with 2 edges per node, {weight , node} (daam phir naam).
//    step 2 : dist[i] = INF for every i, dist[0] = 0, deque dq = { 0 }.
//    step 3 : pop node from the front. for every edge {w , child}:
//             if dist[node] + w < dist[child], set dist[child] = dist[node] + w and
//             push child to the FRONT if w = 0, to the BACK if w = 1.
//    step 4 : return dist[n - 1].
//
// 6. Why it works
//    0-1 bfs = dijkstra where the deque is the priority queue: a 0 edge keeps the same
//    distance (front), a 1 edge is one more (back), so the deque always holds at most two
//    distances d and d + 1 in sorted order, and nodes leave it in order of distance.
//    by 4 the new graph has the same distance to n - 1 as the old one.
//    time O(n): 0-1 bfs is O(nodes + edges) like bfs, and there are only 2n edges. space O(n).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int jump(vector<int>& nums) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        // step 1 : two edges per node, weight first, node second
        vector < vector < pll > > g(n) ;
        for(ll node = 0 ; node < n ; node++)
        {
            g[node].push_back({1 , min(n - 1 , node + nums[node])}) ; // the longest jump
            if(node > 0)
            {
                g[node].push_back({0 , node - 1}) ; // walk back for free
            }
        }
        // step 2
        vector < ll > dist(n , INF) ;
        deque < ll > dq ;
        dist[0] = 0 ;
        dq.push_back(0) ;
        // step 3 : 0-1 bfs
        while(!dq.empty())
        {
            ll node = dq.front() ;
            dq.pop_front() ;
            for(auto [w , child] : g[node])
            {
                if(dist[node] + w < dist[child])
                {
                    dist[child] = dist[node] + w ;
                    if(w == 0)
                    {
                        dq.push_front(child) ;
                    }
                    else
                    {
                        dq.push_back(child) ;
                    }
                }
                else
                {
                    continue ;
                }
            }
        }
        // step 4
        return dist[n - 1] ;
    }
};
274. H-Index Medium

LeetCode · cf/H_Index.cpp

// orz
// 274. H-Index · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// citations[i] is the number of citations of a researcher's i-th paper.
// the h-index is the maximum h such that the researcher has at least h papers
// that each have at least h citations. return the h-index.
// Example 1: citations = [3,0,6,1,5]   -> 3   (3 papers with >= 3 citations: 3, 6, 5)
// Example 2: citations = [1,3,1]       -> 1
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 5000, 0 <= citations[i] <= 1000
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    cnt(h) = number of i with citations[i] >= h.
//    find the largest h in 0 .. n with cnt(h) >= h. (h = 0 always works, h > n never does.)
//
// 2. Brute force
//    try every h from n down to 0, count cnt(h) with a full scan, stop at the first that works.
//    O(n^2) = 2.5 * 10^7. passes here, but it scans the array for every h.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the answer to "does h work?" has a shape, and the brute force ignores it.
//    if h works, every smaller h works too, so most of the h values never need a check.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    monotone: if cnt(h) >= h, then for h' < h, cnt(h') >= cnt(h) >= h > h'
//    (a lower bar lets in at least the same papers). so "h works" is true .. true false .. false,
//    and the largest true is my binary search on answer: lo / hi / ans + bool check.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : check(h) : count the papers with citations >= h, return true if count >= h.
//    step 2 : lo = 0, hi = n, ans = 0 (h = 0 always works).
//    step 3 : mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2. if check(mid), ans = mid and look right: lo = mid + 1.
//             else look left: hi = mid - 1.
//    step 4 : return ans.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    the true h values are exactly 0 .. H (monotone, from 4), and every step keeps H inside
//    lo .. hi or already in ans, so ans ends at H.
//    time O(n log n): log n checks of O(n). extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
// step 1
bool check(ll h , vector<int>& citations)
{
    // this returns true if at least h papers have at least h citations each
    ll n = citations.size() ;
    ll cnt = 0 ;
    for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
    {
        if(citations[i] >= h)
        {
            cnt++ ;
        }
    }
    if(cnt >= h)
    {
        return true ;
    }
    else
    {
        return false ;
    }
}
class Solution {
public:
    int hIndex(vector<int>& citations) {
        ll n = citations.size() ;
        // step 2 : h = 0 always works
        ll lo = 0 , hi = n , ans = 0 ;
        // step 3 : binary search on the answer
        while(lo <= hi)
        {
            ll mid = lo + (hi - lo) / 2 ;
            if(check(mid , citations))
            {
                ans = mid ;
                lo = mid + 1 ;
            }
            else
            {
                hi = mid - 1 ;
            }
        }
        // step 4
        return ans ;
    }
};
380. Insert Delete GetRandom O(1) Medium

LeetCode · cf/Insert_Delete_GetRandom_O_1.cpp

// orz
// 380. Insert Delete GetRandom O(1) · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// implement the class RandomizedSet:
//   RandomizedSet()      initialises an empty set.
//   bool insert(int val) inserts val if it is not present. returns true if it was not present.
//   bool remove(int val) removes val if it is present. returns true if it was present.
//   int getRandom()      returns a random element of the set, each element with the same probability.
// every function must work in average O(1) time.
// Example 1: ["RandomizedSet","insert","remove","insert","getRandom","remove","insert","getRandom"]
//            [[],[1],[2],[2],[],[1],[2],[]]
//            -> [null,true,false,true,1 or 2,true,false,2]
// Constraints: -2^31 <= val <= 2^31 - 1, at most 2 * 10^5 calls in total,
//              getRandom is only called when the set has at least one element.
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    keep a set S under insert / remove, and sample a uniform element of S, each in O(1).
//
// 2. Brute force
//    a vector alone: insert and remove need a search for val, O(n) each.
//    a hash set alone: insert / remove are O(1), but it has no index, so a uniform
//    random element needs a walk of O(n) steps.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the vector has O(1) random access but slow search, the hash set has O(1) search
//    but no random access. each one has exactly what the other one lacks.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    keep both: v = the values in any order (random access), pos[x] = index of x in v
//    (O(1) search). the order inside v doesn't matter, so removing from the middle is
//    "move the last element into the hole, then pop_back", O(1) instead of a shift.
//    (unordered_map, not map: map is O(log n) and the problem asks for O(1).)
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : insert(val) : if val is in pos, return false.
//             else pos[val] = v.size(), push val to the back of v, return true.
//    step 2 : remove(val) : if val is not in pos, return false.
//             else idx = pos[val], last = v.back().
//             move last into the hole: v[idx] = last, pos[last] = idx.
//             then v.pop_back(), erase val from pos, return true.
//             (val == last also works: pos[last] = idx is written, then erased.)
//    step 3 : getRandom() : return v[rand() % v.size()].
//
// 6. Why it works
//    invariant: v holds every element of S exactly once and pos[x] is the index of x in v.
//    insert and remove both keep it, so step 3 picks each element of S with
//    probability 1 / |S|.
//    time average O(1) per call (hash map), space O(n).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class RandomizedSet {
    vector < int > v ;               // this stores the elements, in any order
    unordered_map < int , ll > pos ; // pos[x] = index of x in v
public:
    RandomizedSet() {
    }

    bool insert(int val) {
        // step 1 : already there
        if(pos.find(val) != pos.end())
        {
            return false ;
        }
        else
        {
            pos[val] = v.size() ;
            v.push_back(val) ;
            return true ;
        }
    }

    bool remove(int val) {
        // step 2 : not there
        if(pos.find(val) == pos.end())
        {
            return false ;
        }
        else
        {
            ll idx = pos[val] ;
            int last = v.back() ;
            // move the last element into the hole
            v[idx] = last ;
            pos[last] = idx ;
            v.pop_back() ;
            pos.erase(val) ;
            return true ;
        }
    }

    int getRandom() {
        // step 3 : uniform index into v
        ll idx = rand() % v.size() ;
        return v[idx] ;
    }
};
238. Product of Array Except Self Medium

LeetCode · cf/Product_of_Array_Except_Self.cpp

// orz
// 238. Product of Array Except Self · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// given an integer array nums, return an array answer where answer[i] is the
// product of all the elements of nums except nums[i]. the product of any prefix
// or suffix of nums fits in a 32-bit integer. you must write an O(n) algorithm
// without using division.
// Example 1: nums = [1,2,3,4]       -> [24,12,8,6]
// Example 2: nums = [-1,1,0,-3,3]   -> [0,0,9,0,0]
// Constraints: 2 <= n <= 10^5, -30 <= nums[i] <= 30
// Follow up: O(1) extra space (the output array does not count). -> done here.
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    answer[i] = product of nums[j] over all j != i.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    for every i, multiply every nums[j] with j != i.
//    O(n^2), n = 10^5 gives 10^10. dead.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    answer[i] and answer[i + 1] share almost every factor, and each one
//    multiplies them all again from scratch.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    "every j except i" = "every j left of i" and "every j right of i".
//    answer[i] = L[i] * R[i], where L[i] = nums[0] * .. * nums[i - 1] and
//    R[i] = nums[i + 1] * .. * nums[n - 1]. these are prefix / suffix sums, with * instead of +.
//    (the total / nums[i] trick is banned, and it breaks on a 0 anyway.)
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : walk left to right with a running product left = L[i] (left = 1 at i = 0).
//             write answer[i] = left first, then left *= nums[i].
//             first write, then multiply, because nums[i] must not be in its own product.
//    step 2 : walk right to left with a running product right = R[i] (right = 1 at i = n - 1).
//             answer[i] *= right first, then right *= nums[i]. same order, same reason.
//    step 3 : return answer.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    after step 1 answer[i] = L[i], after step 2 answer[i] = L[i] * R[i] = product of all j != i.
//    left and right are a prefix and a suffix product, so they fit (given).
//    time O(n), extra space O(1): only left and right.
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    vector<int> productExceptSelf(vector<int>& nums) {
        ll n = nums.size() ;
        vector < int > answer(n , 1) ;
        // step 1 : answer[i] = product of everything left of i
        ll left = 1 ;
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            answer[i] = left ;
            left *= nums[i] ;
        }
        // step 2 : multiply in the product of everything right of i
        ll right = 1 ;
        for(ll i = n - 1 ; i >= 0 ; i--)
        {
            answer[i] *= right ;
            right *= nums[i] ;
        }
        // step 3
        return answer ;
    }
};
134. Gas Station Medium

LeetCode · cf/Gas_Station.cpp

// orz
// 134. Gas Station · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// there are n gas stations on a circular route. station i has gas[i] gas, and
// driving from station i to station i + 1 (station n - 1 goes back to 0) costs
// cost[i] gas. the car has an unlimited tank and starts empty at one station.
// return the index of the station where you can start and drive once around the
// circuit clockwise, or -1 if no start works. if an answer exists it is unique.
// Example 1: gas = [1,2,3,4,5], cost = [3,4,5,1,2] -> 3
//            (start at 3: tank 4, 8, 7, 6, 5, back at 3 with 5 >= 0 all the way)
// Example 2: gas = [2,3,4], cost = [3,4,3]           -> -1
// Constraints: n == gas.length == cost.length, 1 <= n <= 10^5,
//              0 <= gas[i], cost[i] <= 10^4
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    let d[k] = gas[k] - cost[k]. find a start s such that for every t = 0 .. n - 1
//    d[s] + d[(s + 1) % n] + .. + d[(s + t) % n] >= 0. if there is none, -1.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    for every start s, drive around and check the tank never goes below 0.
//    n starts, n stations each, O(n^2), n = 10^5 gives 10^10. dead.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    a start i that dies at step T already walked the stations i + 1 .. i + T,
//    and the brute force then starts from i + 1 and walks the same road again.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    say the start i first goes below 0 at step T, at station k = i + T.
//    for every j between i + 1 and k, the car coming from i arrived at j with
//    tank >= 0 (it only died at k). starting fresh at j means arriving with 0,
//    which is no better, so the start j also dies at k or before.
//    so a failed run kills every start inside it: jump straight to k + 1.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : test(i) drives from i. at step t it is at station (i + t) % n and adds
//             gas - cost of that station. it returns the first t where the tank is
//             below 0, or n when the car makes it all the way around.
//    step 2 : i = 0. while i < n : T = test(i).
//             if T == n, i is the answer.
//             else every start i .. i + T dies, so i = i + T + 1.
//    step 3 : i ran past n - 1, so every start died : return -1.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    step 4 shows the skipped starts all die, and the starts before i died earlier,
//    so when test(i) == n, i is the only possible answer (it is unique, given).
//    the failed tests walk disjoint stretches of stations (i jumps past each one),
//    so they cost n in total, and the one successful test costs n more.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
ll test(ll i , vector<int>& gas , vector<int>& cost)
{
    // this returns the first step t where the tank goes below 0 starting at station i,
    // or n if the car goes all the way around
    ll n = gas.size() ;
    ll tank = 0 ;
    for(ll t = 0 ; t < n ; t++)
    {
        ll k = (i + t) % n ;
        tank += gas[k] - cost[k] ;
        if(tank < 0)
        {
            return t ;
        }
    }
    return n ;
}
class Solution {
public:
    int canCompleteCircuit(vector<int>& gas, vector<int>& cost) {
        ll n = gas.size() ;
        // step 2 : test i, jump past the whole stretch it died on
        ll i = 0 ;
        while(i < n)
        {
            // step 1
            ll T = test(i , gas , cost) ;
            if(T == n)
            {
                return i ;
            }
            else
            {
                i = i + T + 1 ;
            }
        }
        // step 3 : every start died
        return -1 ;
    }
};
135. Candy Hard

LeetCode · cf/Candy.cpp

// orz
// 135. Candy · LeetCode · Hard
//
// Question:
// n children stand in a line and ratings[i] is the rating of child i. give them
// candies so that every child gets at least one candy, and a child with a higher
// rating than a neighbour gets more candies than that neighbour.
// return the minimum total number of candies.
// Example 1: ratings = [1,0,2] -> 5   (2, 1, 2)
// Example 2: ratings = [1,2,2] -> 4   (1, 2, 1 : equal neighbours need nothing)
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 2 * 10^4, 0 <= ratings[i] <= 2 * 10^4
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    find c[0 .. n - 1] with c[i] >= 1,
//    a[i] > a[i - 1] => c[i] > c[i - 1] (left rule),
//    a[i] > a[i + 1] => c[i] > c[i + 1] (right rule),
//    and minimise c[0] + .. + c[n - 1].
//
// 2. Brute force
//    start everyone at 1. sweep the line and fix every broken rule by giving
//    c[i] = c[neighbour] + 1. repeat sweeps until nothing changes.
//    a long decreasing run fixes one child per sweep, so O(n^2), 4 * 10^8. dead.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    each sweep pushes the information only one child further along the line,
//    and both rules get mixed in the same sweep.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    split the two rules. the left rule only looks left, so one left to right pass
//    settles it: L[i] = L[i - 1] + 1 when a[i] > a[i - 1], else 1.
//    the right rule only looks right: R[i] = R[i + 1] + 1 when a[i] > a[i + 1], else 1.
//    a child must satisfy both, so c[i] = max(L[i] , R[i]).
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : L[i] = length of the increasing run ending at i, walking left to right.
//    step 2 : R[i] = length of the decreasing run starting at i, walking right to left.
//    step 3 : ans = sum of max(L[i] , R[i]).
//
// 6. Why it works
//    lower bound : any valid c has c[i] >= L[i] (induction along the left rule,
//    c[i] > c[i - 1] >= L[i - 1]) and c[i] >= R[i] (same from the right).
//    valid : if a[i] > a[i - 1] then R[i - 1] = 1 (a[i - 1] is not bigger than a[i]),
//    so c[i - 1] = L[i - 1] < L[i] <= c[i]. the right rule is the mirror.
//    so max(L , R) is valid and every valid c is at least it : it is the minimum.
//    time O(n), extra space O(n).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int candy(vector<int>& ratings) {
        ll n = ratings.size() ;
        // step 1 : L[i] stores the candies child i needs for the left rule alone
        vector < ll > L(n , 1) ;
        for(ll i = 1 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            if(ratings[i] > ratings[i - 1])
            {
                L[i] = L[i - 1] + 1 ;
            }
            else
            {
                continue ;
            }
        }
        // step 2 : R[i] stores the candies child i needs for the right rule alone
        vector < ll > R(n , 1) ;
        for(ll i = n - 2 ; i >= 0 ; i--)
        {
            if(ratings[i] > ratings[i + 1])
            {
                R[i] = R[i + 1] + 1 ;
            }
            else
            {
                continue ;
            }
        }
        // step 3 : both rules at once
        ll ans = 0 ;
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            ans += max(L[i] , R[i]) ;
        }
        return ans ;
    }
};
42. Trapping Rain Water Hard

LeetCode · cf/Trapping_Rain_Water.cpp

// orz
// 42. Trapping Rain Water · LeetCode · Hard
//
// Question:
// given n non-negative integers height[i], the heights of bars of width 1 standing
// side by side, return how much water is trapped between them after raining.
// Example 1: height = [0,1,0,2,1,0,1,3,2,1,2,1] -> 6
// Example 2: height = [4,2,0,3,2,5]             -> 9
// Constraints: 1 <= n <= 2 * 10^4, 0 <= height[i] <= 10^5
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    water[i] = max(0 , min(max of h[0 .. i - 1] , max of h[i + 1 .. n - 1]) - h[i]).
//    the two end bars hold nothing. answer = sum of water[i].
//
// 2. Brute force
//    for every i, scan left for the tallest bar and scan right for the tallest bar.
//    O(n^2), n = 2 * 10^4 gives 4 * 10^8. dead.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the tallest bar left of i and left of i + 1 differ by one bar only,
//    and each scan finds it again from scratch.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    "tallest bar on the left of i" is a prefix max, "tallest on the right" a suffix max.
//    the same prefix / suffix idea as Product of Array Except Self, with max instead of *.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : dp1[i] = max of h[0 .. i], left to right : dp1[i] = max(h[i] , dp1[i - 1]).
//    step 2 : dp2[i] = max of h[i .. n - 1], right to left : dp2[i] = max(h[i] , dp2[i + 1]).
//    step 3 : for i = 1 .. n - 2, water = min(dp1[i - 1] , dp2[i + 1]) - h[i].
//             if it is below 0 (bar i is taller than a side) the bar holds 0. add it up.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    water above bar i rises until it spills over the lower of the two tallest walls
//    around it, so the level is min(left wall , right wall) and the depth is that minus h[i].
//    time O(n), extra space O(n).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int trap(vector<int>& height) {
        ll n = height.size() ;
        // step 1 : dp1[i] stores the tallest bar in 0 .. i
        vector < ll > dp1(n , 0) ;
        dp1[0] = height[0] ;
        for(ll i = 1 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            dp1[i] = max((ll)height[i] , dp1[i - 1]) ;
        }
        // step 2 : dp2[i] stores the tallest bar in i .. n - 1
        vector < ll > dp2(n , 0) ;
        dp2[n - 1] = height[n - 1] ;
        for(ll i = n - 2 ; i >= 0 ; i--)
        {
            dp2[i] = max((ll)height[i] , dp2[i + 1]) ;
        }
        // step 3 : water above every inner bar
        ll ans = 0 ;
        for(ll i = 1 ; i < n - 1 ; i++)
        {
            ll water = min(dp1[i - 1] , dp2[i + 1]) - height[i] ;
            if(water < 0)
            {
                water = 0 ;
            }
            ans += water ;
        }
        return ans ;
    }
};
13. Roman to Integer Easy

LeetCode · cf/Roman_to_Integer.cpp

// orz
// 13. Roman to Integer · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// roman numerals use I = 1, V = 5, X = 10, L = 50, C = 100, D = 500, M = 1000.
// symbols are written largest to smallest and added, except six subtractive pairs:
// IV = 4, IX = 9, XL = 40, XC = 90, CD = 400, CM = 900.
// given a roman numeral s, return its value.
// Example 1: s = "III"     -> 3
// Example 2: s = "LVIII"   -> 58    (L = 50, V = 5, III = 3)
// Example 3: s = "MCMXCIV" -> 1994  (M = 1000, CM = 900, XC = 90, IV = 4)
// Constraints: 1 <= s.length <= 15, s has only I V X L C D M,
//              s is a valid roman numeral in [1 , 3999]
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    value(s) = sum over i of sign[i] * val(s[i]), where sign[i] = -1 if
//    val(s[i]) < val(s[i + 1]) (s[i] is the small half of a subtractive pair), else +1.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    write the roman numeral of every number 1 .. 3999 and return the one equal to s.
//    3999 numerals of up to 15 chars each. it works, but it builds 3999 strings to read one.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the value can be read straight off s. a symbol's contribution only depends on
//    the symbol right after it.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    in a valid numeral a smaller symbol before a bigger one only happens inside
//    a subtractive pair. so: smaller than the next symbol -> subtract, else add.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : val(ch) gives the value of one symbol.
//    step 2 : walk i = 0 .. n - 1. if i + 1 < n and val(s[i]) < val(s[i + 1]),
//             subtract val(s[i]). else add it.
//    step 3 : return the total.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    symbols go largest to smallest except the pairs, so a dip upward only happens at
//    the first symbol of a pair. subtracting it and adding the second gives exactly
//    the pair value (IV = -1 + 5 = 4). every other symbol is added as is.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
ll val(char ch)
{
    // this returns the value of one roman symbol
    if(ch == 'I')
    {
        return 1 ;
    }
    else if(ch == 'V')
    {
        return 5 ;
    }
    else if(ch == 'X')
    {
        return 10 ;
    }
    else if(ch == 'L')
    {
        return 50 ;
    }
    else if(ch == 'C')
    {
        return 100 ;
    }
    else if(ch == 'D')
    {
        return 500 ;
    }
    else
    {
        return 1000 ;
    }
}
class Solution {
public:
    int romanToInt(string s) {
        ll n = s.size() ;
        ll ans = 0 ;
        // step 2 : subtract a symbol smaller than the next one, add the rest
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            // step 1
            ll cur = val(s[i]) ;
            if(i + 1 < n && cur < val(s[i + 1]))
            {
                ans -= cur ;
            }
            else
            {
                ans += cur ;
            }
        }
        // step 3
        return ans ;
    }
};
12. Integer to Roman Medium

LeetCode · cf/Integer_to_Roman.cpp

// orz
// 12. Integer to Roman · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// roman numerals use I = 1, V = 5, X = 10, L = 50, C = 100, D = 500, M = 1000.
// a number is written one decimal place at a time, from thousands down to ones:
// a digit 4 or 9 uses the subtractive form (IV = 4, IX = 9, XL = 40, XC = 90,
// CD = 400, CM = 900); any other digit uses the 5-symbol (V, L, D) at most once
// and the 1-symbol (I, X, C, M) at most 3 times. given num, return its roman numeral.
// Example 1: num = 3749 -> "MMMDCCXLIX"  (MMM + DCC + XL + IX)
// Example 2: num = 58   -> "LVIII"       (L + VIII)
// Example 3: num = 1994 -> "MCMXCIV"     (M + CM + XC + IV)
// Constraints: 1 <= num <= 3999
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    num = 1000 * a + 100 * b + 10 * c + d. write a, b, c, d one after the other,
//    each with its place's symbols : digit 9 -> (one)(ten), 5 .. 8 -> (five) + (digit - 5) ones,
//    4 -> (one)(five), 0 .. 3 -> that many ones.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    hand-write all four cases for every place : 4 places * 10 digits = 40 strings,
//    and glue the four lookups together.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the 40 strings are the same 4 patterns repeated with different letters.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    there are only 13 "chunks" : 1000 M, 900 CM, 500 D, 400 CD, 100 C, 90 XC,
//    50 L, 40 XL, 10 X, 9 IX, 5 V, 4 IV, 1 I.
//    greedy : take the biggest chunk that still fits, write it, subtract, repeat.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : the 13 chunks, biggest first, in val[] and sym[].
//    step 2 : for every chunk k, while num >= val[k], append sym[k] and num -= val[k].
//    step 3 : return the string.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    take one place, say the hundreds digit b. the greedy first takes 900 if b = 9,
//    else 500 if b >= 5, else 400 if b = 4, then 100s for the rest. that is exactly the
//    digit rule above. a chunk of a place never fits after that place is done
//    (what is left is below 100), so the places come out one after the other.
//    at most 15 symbols, time O(1), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    string intToRoman(int num) {
        // step 1 : the 13 chunks, biggest first
        vector < ll > val = {1000 , 900 , 500 , 400 , 100 , 90 , 50 , 40 , 10 , 9 , 5 , 4 , 1} ;
        vector < string > sym = {"M" , "CM" , "D" , "CD" , "C" , "XC" , "L" , "XL" , "X" , "IX" , "V" , "IV" , "I"} ;
        // step 2 : biggest chunk that still fits
        ll x = num ;
        string ans = "" ;
        for(ll k = 0 ; k < 13 ; k++)
        {
            while(x >= val[k])
            {
                ans += sym[k] ;
                x -= val[k] ;
            }
        }
        // step 3
        return ans ;
    }
};
58. Length of Last Word Easy

LeetCode · cf/Length_of_Last_Word.cpp

// orz
// 58. Length of Last Word · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// given a string s of words and spaces, return the length of the last word.
// a word is a maximal run of non-space characters.
// Example 1: s = "Hello World"                 -> 5  ("World")
// Example 2: s = "   fly me   to   the moon  " -> 4  ("moon")
// Example 3: s = "luffy is still joyboy"       -> 6  ("joyboy")
// Constraints: 1 <= s.length <= 10^4, s has only English letters and spaces,
//              there is at least one word in s
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    let e = the last index with s[e] != ' ', and b = the last index < e with s[b] == ' '
//    (b = -1 if there is none). answer = e - b.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    split s into all its words, store them, return the size of the last one.
//    O(n) time but O(n) extra space for words we never look at.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    every word but the last is stored for nothing.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    the last word sits at the end, so read s backwards : skip the trailing spaces,
//    then count letters until the next space.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : i = n - 1. while s[i] is a space, i--.
//    step 2 : while i >= 0 and s[i] is not a space, len++ and i--.
//    step 3 : return len.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    after step 1, s[i] is the last letter of the last word (there is at least one word).
//    step 2 walks exactly that word back to its start.
//    time O(n), extra space O(1).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int lengthOfLastWord(string s) {
        ll n = s.size() ;
        // step 1 : skip the trailing spaces
        ll i = n - 1 ;
        while(i >= 0 && s[i] == ' ')
        {
            i-- ;
        }
        // step 2 : count the letters of the last word
        ll len = 0 ;
        while(i >= 0 && s[i] != ' ')
        {
            len++ ;
            i-- ;
        }
        // step 3
        return len ;
    }
};
14. Longest Common Prefix Easy

LeetCode · cf/Longest_Common_Prefix.cpp

// orz
// 14. Longest Common Prefix · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// given an array of strings strs, return the longest string that is a prefix of
// all of them. if there is none, return "".
// Example 1: strs = ["flower","flow","flight"]  -> "fl"
// Example 2: strs = ["dog","racecar","car"]     -> ""
// Constraints: 1 <= strs.length <= 200, 0 <= strs[i].length <= 200,
//              strs[i] has only lowercase English letters
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    find the largest L such that for every string w in strs, |w| >= L and
//    w[j] == strs[0][j] for all j < L. return strs[0][0 .. L - 1].
//
// 2. Brute force
//    for L = |strs[0]| down to 0, check whether every string starts with strs[0][0 .. L - 1],
//    return the first L that works. each check costs n * L : O(n * m^2) with m = 200.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the check for L and for L - 1 compare the same first L - 1 columns again.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    the condition is column by column : column j is good if every string has a
//    j-th char equal to strs[0][j]. the answer is the first bad column.
//    so scan columns j = 0, 1, 2 .. and stop at the first bad one.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : for j = 0, 1, .. up to |strs[0]| - 1, ch = strs[0][j].
//    step 2 : for every string w, if |w| <= j or w[j] != ch, column j is bad : return the
//             prefix strs[0][0 .. j - 1].
//    step 3 : the column is good, append ch to the answer and go to j + 1.
//    step 4 : strs[0] ran out with every column good : the answer is all of strs[0].
//
// 6. Why it works
//    the prefix of length L is common iff columns 0 .. L - 1 are all good, so the first
//    bad column is exactly the answer length. each char is compared at most once.
//    time O(total chars), extra space O(1) besides the answer.
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    string longestCommonPrefix(vector<string>& strs) {
        ll n = strs.size() ;
        ll m = strs[0].size() ;
        string ans = "" ;
        // step 1 : column j
        for(ll j = 0 ; j < m ; j++)
        {
            char ch = strs[0][j] ;
            // step 2 : every string must have ch at column j
            for(ll k = 0 ; k < n ; k++)
            {
                ll len = strs[k].size() ;
                if(len <= j || strs[k][j] != ch)
                {
                    return ans ;
                }
                else
                {
                    continue ;
                }
            }
            // step 3 : column j is good
            ans += ch ;
        }
        // step 4
        return ans ;
    }
};
151. Reverse Words in a String Medium

LeetCode · cf/Reverse_Words_in_a_String.cpp

// orz
// 151. Reverse Words in a String · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// given a string s, reverse the order of its words. a word is a maximal run of
// non-space characters. s may have leading, trailing or multiple spaces between
// words; the answer has the words in reverse order, joined by single spaces,
// with no leading or trailing space.
// Example 1: s = "the sky is blue"  -> "blue is sky the"
// Example 2: s = "  hello world  "  -> "world hello"
// Example 3: s = "a good   example" -> "example good a"
// Constraints: 1 <= s.length <= 10^4, s has English letters (upper and lower case),
//              digits and spaces ' ', there is at least one word in s
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    let w[0], w[1], .. , w[k - 1] be the words of s from left to right.
//    return w[k - 1] + " " + w[k - 2] + " " + .. + " " + w[0].
//
// 2. Brute force
//    find the last word of s, append it to the answer, erase it from s, repeat.
//    each erase shifts the rest of s, so O(n^2).
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    it searches s from the end again for every word, and erasing copies the string
//    every time.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    reading the words left to right once gives the list w. reversing the order is then
//    just reading the list from its back.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : walk s with i. skip spaces. at a letter, read the whole word
//             (until a space or the end) and push it to words.
//    step 2 : walk words from the back, append each one, and a single space between two words.
//    step 3 : return the answer.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    step 1 collects exactly the maximal runs of non-spaces, in order, and drops every space.
//    step 2 puts them back in reverse order with exactly one space between neighbours.
//    every char is touched a constant number of times : time O(n), extra space O(n).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    string reverseWords(string s) {
        ll n = s.size() ;
        // step 1 : words stores the words of s from left to right
        vector < string > words ;
        ll i = 0 ;
        while(i < n)
        {
            if(s[i] == ' ')
            {
                i++ ;
                continue ;
            }
            string word = "" ;
            while(i < n && s[i] != ' ')
            {
                word += s[i] ;
                i++ ;
            }
            words.push_back(word) ;
        }
        // step 2 : words from the back, one space between two words
        ll k = words.size() ;
        string ans = "" ;
        for(ll j = k - 1 ; j >= 0 ; j--)
        {
            ans += words[j] ;
            if(j > 0)
            {
                ans += ' ' ;
            }
        }
        // step 3
        return ans ;
    }
};
6. Zigzag Conversion Medium

LeetCode · cf/Zigzag_Conversion.cpp

// orz
// 6. Zigzag Conversion · LeetCode · Medium
//
// Question:
// write the string s in a zigzag on numRows rows : go down the rows one char at a
// time, then diagonally back up, then down again, and so on. then read the rows
// top to bottom, each left to right, and return that string.
// "PAYPALISHIRING" on 3 rows :
//     P   A   H   N
//     A P L S I I G
//     Y   I   R
// Example 1: s = "PAYPALISHIRING", numRows = 3 -> "PAHNAPLSIIGYIR"
// Example 2: s = "PAYPALISHIRING", numRows = 4 -> "PINALSIGYAHRPI"
// Example 3: s = "A", numRows = 1              -> "A"
// Constraints: 1 <= s.length <= 1000, s has English letters (upper and lower case),
//              ',' and '.', 1 <= numRows <= 1000
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    the row of char i is r(i) : r(0) = 0, and r goes +1 per char until it hits
//    numRows - 1, then -1 until it hits 0, and so on.
//    return all chars with r = 0 (in order), then r = 1, .. , then r = numRows - 1.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    build the full 2D grid numRows x n of blanks, put every char in its cell,
//    then read the grid row by row skipping blanks. O(numRows * n) = 10^6 cells,
//    almost all of them blank.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the column of a char never matters : inside one row the chars are already in
//    the order of s. only the row matters.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    keep one string per row. walk s once, append each char to its row's string,
//    and move the row like a ball bouncing between 0 and numRows - 1.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : numRows == 1 : there is no zigzag, return s (the row could not bounce).
//    step 2 : rows[0 .. numRows - 1] empty strings, r = 0, dir = +1.
//    step 3 : for every char : rows[r] += ch. if r == 0, dir = +1.
//             if r == numRows - 1, dir = -1. then r += dir.
//    step 4 : glue rows[0], rows[1], .. , rows[numRows - 1] and return.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    step 3 gives each char exactly the row r(i) of the statement, and inside a row
//    chars are appended in the order of s, which is the left to right order in the grid.
//    time O(n), extra space O(n).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    string convert(string s, int numRows) {
        ll n = s.size() ;
        ll k = numRows ;
        // step 1 : one row, nothing moves
        if(k == 1)
        {
            return s ;
        }
        // step 2 : rows[r] stores the chars of row r in order
        vector < string > rows(k , "") ;
        ll r = 0 ;
        ll dir = 1 ;
        // step 3 : bounce between row 0 and row k - 1
        for(ll i = 0 ; i < n ; i++)
        {
            rows[r] += s[i] ;
            if(r == 0)
            {
                dir = 1 ;
            }
            else if(r == k - 1)
            {
                dir = -1 ;
            }
            r += dir ;
        }
        // step 4 : read the rows top to bottom
        string ans = "" ;
        for(ll j = 0 ; j < k ; j++)
        {
            ans += rows[j] ;
        }
        return ans ;
    }
};
28. Find the Index of the First Occurrence in a String Easy

LeetCode · cf/Find_the_Index_of_the_First_Occurrence_in_a_String.cpp

// orz
// 28. Find the Index of the First Occurrence in a String · LeetCode · Easy
//
// Question:
// given two strings haystack and needle, return the index of the first occurrence
// of needle in haystack, or -1 if needle is not part of haystack.
// Example 1: haystack = "sadbutsad", needle = "sad"  -> 0  (at 0 and 6, the first is 0)
// Example 2: haystack = "leetcode", needle = "leeto" -> -1
// Constraints: 1 <= haystack.length, needle.length <= 10^4,
//              both have only lowercase English letters
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    n = |haystack|, m = |needle|. find the smallest i, 0 <= i <= n - m, with
//    haystack[i + j] == needle[j] for all j < m. if there is none, -1.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    try every start i and compare up to m chars.
//    O(n * m), 10^4 * 10^4 = 10^8 char compares in the worst case (haystack = "aaa..a",
//    needle = "aa..ab").
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    after matching j chars and failing, it moves the start by one and compares
//    chars it has already seen. those j chars were needle[0 .. j - 1], so we already
//    know what they are.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    prefix function (KMP). glue t = needle + '#' + haystack ('#' is in neither string).
//    pi[i] = the length of the longest proper prefix of t[0 .. i] that is also a suffix of it.
//    the prefix of t is (part of) needle, and '#' stops it from ever going past m.
//    so pi[i] == m means : needle ends at position i of t.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : t = needle + '#' + haystack, len = m + 1 + n, pi[0] = 0.
//    step 2 : for i = 1 .. len - 1 : k = pi[i - 1] (the border I am extending).
//             while k > 0 and t[i] != t[k], fall back k = pi[k - 1] (the next shorter border).
//             if t[i] == t[k], k++. pi[i] = k.
//    step 3 : the first i with pi[i] == m : needle ends at i of t, so it starts at
//             i - m + 1 in t, which is i - m + 1 - (m + 1) = i - 2 * m in haystack. return it.
//    step 4 : no such i : return -1.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    a border of t[0 .. i] that can be extended by t[i] is a border of t[0 .. i - 1]
//    followed by t[i], and the borders of t[0 .. i - 1] are exactly pi[i - 1],
//    pi[pi[i - 1] - 1], .. (each one is the longest border of the one before).
//    so step 2 tries every candidate, longest first. k grows by at most 1 per i and every
//    fall back makes it smaller, so the falls back cost at most len in total.
//    time O(n + m), extra space O(n + m).
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    int strStr(string haystack, string needle) {
        ll m = needle.size() ;
        // step 1 : needle, a separator, then haystack
        string t = needle + '#' + haystack ;
        ll len = t.size() ;
        // pi[i] stores the longest proper prefix of t[0 .. i] that is also its suffix
        vector < ll > pi(len , 0) ;
        for(ll i = 1 ; i < len ; i++)
        {
            // step 2 : extend the border of t[0 .. i - 1]
            ll k = pi[i - 1] ;
            while(k > 0 && t[i] != t[k])
            {
                k = pi[k - 1] ;
            }
            if(t[i] == t[k])
            {
                k++ ;
            }
            pi[i] = k ;
            // step 3 : needle ends at i
            if(pi[i] == m)
            {
                return i - 2 * m ;
            }
        }
        // step 4
        return -1 ;
    }
};
68. Text Justification Hard

LeetCode · cf/Text_Justification.cpp

// orz
// 68. Text Justification · LeetCode · Hard
//
// Question:
// given an array of words and a width maxWidth, format the text so every line has
// exactly maxWidth characters. pack words greedily : put as many words as fit on
// each line (one space at least between two words). pad with extra spaces so the
// line is exactly maxWidth wide, spread as evenly as possible between the words;
// if they do not split evenly, the gaps on the left get one more space than the
// gaps on the right. the last line, and any line with a single word, is left
// justified : single spaces between words, the rest of the spaces at the end.
// Example 1: words = ["This", "is", "an", "example", "of", "text", "justification."], maxWidth = 16
//            -> ["This    is    an",
//                "example  of text",
//                "justification.  "]
// Example 2: words = ["What","must","be","acknowledgment","shall","be"], maxWidth = 16
//            -> ["What   must   be",
//                "acknowledgment  ",
//                "shall be        "]
// Example 3: words = ["Science","is","what","we","understand","well","enough","to","explain",
//                     "to","a","computer.","Art","is","everything","else","we","do"], maxWidth = 20
//            -> ["Science  is  what we",
//                "understand      well",
//                "enough to explain to",
//                "a  computer.  Art is",
//                "everything  else  we",
//                "do                  "]
// Constraints: 1 <= words.length <= 300, 1 <= words[i].length <= 20,
//              words[i] has only English letters and symbols (no spaces),
//              1 <= maxWidth <= 100, words[i].length <= maxWidth
//
// 1. Mathematical statement
//    a line holds words t .. h when (sum of their lengths) + (h - t) <= maxWidth, and
//    greedy means h is the largest such index. for a line with g = h - t gaps and
//    sp = maxWidth - (sum of lengths) spaces : if it is the last line or g = 0, single spaces
//    then sp - g spaces at the end. else every gap gets sp / g spaces, and the first
//    sp % g gaps get one more.
//
// 2. Brute force
//    for every line, try every end h = t, t + 1, .. and add up the lengths of t .. h from
//    scratch each time, keep the largest h that fits. O(n^2) adding for the packing.
//
// 3. What the brute force wastes
//    the sum for t .. h + 1 is the sum for t .. h plus one word, and it recomputes it.
//
// 4. Key breaking point
//    a line is a window [t , h] that only grows while the next word fits : my sliding
//    window t / h, with a running width. the next line starts at h + 1.
//    the spacing is then the statement turned into numbers : sp / g and sp % g.
//
// 5. Method
//    step 1 : t = 0. while t < n : h = t, width = |words[t]|.
//             while h + 1 < n and width + 1 + |words[h + 1]| <= maxWidth : h++, width += 1 + |words[h]|.
//    step 2 : g = h - t gaps, letters = width - g (the single spaces taken out),
//             sp = maxWidth - letters.
//    step 3 : last line (h == n - 1) or one word (g == 0) : words with single spaces,
//             then pad the end with spaces up to maxWidth.
//    step 4 : else base = sp / g, extra = sp % g. after word t + j (j < g) put base spaces,
//             plus one more when j < extra.
//    step 5 : push the line, t = h + 1.
//
// 6. Why it works
//    step 1 is greedy packing : it stops exactly when one more word plus its space would
//    overflow. steps 3 and 4 are the spacing rules word for word : base * g + extra = sp, so
//    the line is exactly maxWidth, and the left gaps get the extra ones.
//    every word is added once and every output char written once : time O(n * maxWidth),
//    extra space O(1) besides the answer.
#include <bits/stdc++.h>
#define ll long long
#define endl "\n"
#define fr first
#define sc second
#define MOD 1000000007
#define MOD1 998244353
#define INF 1e18
#define mp make_pair
#define PI acos(-1)
#define py cout << "YES" << endl
#define pn cout << "NO" << endl
#define pll pair<ll,ll>
using namespace std;
class Solution {
public:
    vector<string> fullJustify(vector<string>& words, int maxWidth) {
        ll n = words.size() ;
        ll w = maxWidth ;
        vector < string > ans ;
        ll t = 0 ;
        while(t < n)
        {
            // step 1 : grow the line [t , h] while the next word still fits
            ll h = t ;
            ll width = words[t].size() ; // this stores the words t .. h with single spaces
            while(h + 1 < n && width + 1 + (ll)words[h + 1].size() <= w)
            {
                h++ ;
                width += 1 + words[h].size() ;
            }
            // step 2 : letters, spaces, gaps
            ll g = h - t ;
            ll letters = width - g ;
            ll sp = w - letters ;
            string line = "" ;
            if(h == n - 1 || g == 0)
            {
                // step 3 : left justified
                for(ll j = t ; j <= h ; j++)
                {
                    line += words[j] ;
                    if(j < h)
                    {
                        line += ' ' ;
                    }
                }
                while((ll)line.size() < w)
                {
                    line += ' ' ;
                }
            }
            else
            {
                // step 4 : base spaces per gap, the first extra gaps get one more
                ll base = sp / g ;
                ll extra = sp % g ;
                for(ll j = 0 ; j <= g ; j++)
                {
                    line += words[t + j] ;
                    if(j == g)
                    {
                        continue ;
                    }
                    ll cnt = base ;
